s,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
with
\sigma_{1}\leq\sigma_{2}\leq\frac{k}{2}

such that

2k-2\sigma_{1}-2\sigma_{2}>1
and

(5.29)

\displaystyle s<\begin{cases}k-\sigma_{1}-\sigma_{2}-1,&\text{if }k-2\sigma_{1}<1,\\
\frac{1}{2}k-\sigma_{2}-\frac{1}{2},&\text{if }k-2\sigma_{1}\geq 1.\end{cases}

Then, given any finite
p\geq 1
,
1\leq r\leq\infty
,
and
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\{0,1\}
, we have

(5.30)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\big\|\mathbf{P}_{\neq 0}\big((\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{\sigma_{1}}\mathcal{H}^{\alpha_{1}}\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})(\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{\sigma_{2}}\mathcal{H}^{\alpha_{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})\big)\big\|_{W^{s,r}}^{p}\bigg]<C_{\delta}<\infty,

uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
where the constant
C_{\delta}>0
is independent of
2\leq\delta\leq\infty
.

Remark 5.10
.

The bound (
5.30
) also holds
even if we replace

\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{\sigma_{j}}
by
\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{\sigma_{j}-\kappa_{j}}\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{j}}

for any
\kappa_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
with
\kappa_{j}\leq\sigma_{j}
,
j=1,2
.

Proof.

We only present the case
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}=0

since the other cases can be treated in a similar manner.
Proceeding as in the proof of Proposition
5.1
(i)
with Lemma
2.8
, it suffices to consider the case
p=r=2
.
We first consider the case
k-2\sigma_{1}\neq 1
.
From (
1.26
),
(
5.1
), and
Lemma
2.7
, we have

LHS of (
5.30
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\langle n\rangle^{2s}\bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n=n_{1}+n_{2}\neq 0\\
0<|n_{j}|\leq N\end{subarray}}\frac{g_{n_{1}}g_{n_{2}}}{(T_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(n_{1})T_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(n_{2}))^{\frac{1}{2}}}\langle n_{1}\rangle^{\sigma_{1}}\langle n_{2}\rangle^{\sigma_{2}}\bigg|^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\sim_{\delta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle n\rangle^{2s}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n=n_{1}+n_{2}\\
0<|n_{j}|\leq N\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\langle n_{1}\rangle^{k-2\sigma_{1}}\langle n_{2}\rangle^{k-2\sigma_{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{\langle n\rangle^{-2s+k-2\sigma_{2}+\min(k-2\sigma_{1}-1,0)}}<\infty,

provided that (
5.29
) holds,
where the implicit constant can be chosen to be independent
of
2\leq\delta\leq\infty
.
When
k-2\sigma_{1}=1
, in view of Lemma
2.7
,
essentially the same computation
as above holds, yielding (
5.30
) under (
5.29
).
∎

We now present a proof of
Lemma
5.6
.

Proof of Lemma
5.6
.

Fix an integer
k\geq 2
.
We first consider the case
\ell=1
.
From
(
3.65
),
Sobolev’s inequality, (
2.3
), and Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle|A_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{1}{2}}(u_{1}+u_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|u_{1}\|^{3}_{L^{3}}+\|u_{2}\|^{\frac{3k-1}{k}}_{L^{2}}\|u_{2}\|^{\frac{1}{k}}_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon_{0}}\Big(\|u_{1}\|_{W^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon,\infty}}^{3}+\|u_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2(3k-1)}{2k-1}}\Big)+\varepsilon_{0}\|u_{2}\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}

for any
\varepsilon_{0}>0
.

In the following, we consider the case
2\leq\ell\leq k-1
,
which in particular implies
k\geq 3
.

\bullet

Case 1:

\ell=2m
for some
1\leq m\leq\frac{k-1}{2}
.

Let us consider the first term on the right-hand side of (
3.63
).
Noting that
\#\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}=0
, it
suffices to consider a monomial of the form

p(u)=u\partial_{x}^{m-1}u\partial_{x}^{m}u

thanks to
Lemma
3.9
and
the
L^{r}
-boundedness of the Hilbert transform
\mathcal{H}

for
1<r<\infty
(Lemma
2.4
(iii)).
28
28

28

For the terms with the
W^{s,\infty}
-norm,
we can instead work with the
W^{s,r}
-norm with
r\gg 1

to exploit the boundedness of the Hilbert transform.
This may cause an
\varepsilon
-loss of regularity (via Sobolev’s inequality;
see, for example, (
5.34
))
but does not affect the end result.
Thus, for simplicity of the presentation, we work with
the
W^{s,\infty}
-norm in the following.
The same comment applies to the proofs of Lemmas
5.7

and
6.7
.

By crudely estimating with
(fractional) integration by parts,
Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality,
and the fractional Leibniz rule (
2.4
),
we have

(5.31)

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{T}}u\partial_{x}^{m-1}u\partial_{x}^{m}udx\bigg|\leq\|u\partial_{x}^{m-1}u\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}\|u\|_{H^{m-\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim\|u\|_{W^{\frac{1}{4},\infty}}\|u\|_{H^{\frac{2k-3}{4}}}^{2}.

On the one hand, we have

(5.32)

\displaystyle\|u\|_{W^{\frac{1}{4},\infty}}+\|u\|_{H^{\frac{2k-3}{4}}}\lesssim\|u\|_{W^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon,\infty}}.

On the other hand, by
Sobolev inequality and
(
2.3
), we have

(5.33)

\displaystyle\|u\|_{W^{\frac{1}{4},\infty}}\|u\|_{H^{\frac{2k-3}{4}}}^{2}\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{\frac{3+\varepsilon}{4}}}\|u\|_{H^{\frac{2k-3}{4}}}^{2}\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{3-\gamma}\|u\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{\gamma},

where

\gamma=\frac{3+\varepsilon}{2k}+\frac{2k-3}{k}<2
.
Hence, the bound (
5.23
) in this case follows from substituting
u=u_{1}+u_{2}
into
the left-hand side of (
5.31
)
and applying
multilinearity of
p(u)
,
(
5.32
), (
5.33
), and Young’s inequality.

Next, we consider
the second term on the right-hand side of (
3.63
).
Recall from Definition
3.7

that a monomial
p(u)\in\mathcal{P}_{j}(u)

comes with the Hilbert transform
\mathcal{H}

and the perturbation operator
\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
in (
1.14
)
over various factors in a
nested
manner.
In the following, we first apply Hölder’s inequality iteratively
to estimate
\int_{\mathbb{T}}p(u)dx

and, in doing so,
we can simply drop
the Hilbert transform
\mathcal{H}

and the perturbation operator
\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
,
thanks to their
L^{r}
-boundedness
for
1<r<\infty
(Lemma
2.4
(iii)).
29
29

29

As pointed out above, the
W^{s,\infty}
-norm in (
5.34
)
should really be the
W^{s,r}
-norm with
r\gg 1
.
For simplicity of the presentation, however, we work with
the
W^{s,\infty}
-norm in the following.

Thus, the contribution from
the second term on the right-hand side of (
3.63
)
is bounded by

(5.34)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&C_{\delta}\sum_{j=3}^{2m+2}\sum_{\#\partial_{x}=0}^{2m+2-j}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{j}=0\\
\alpha_{1\cdots j}=\#\partial_{x}\end{subarray}}^{m-1}\|u\|_{H^{\alpha_{1}}}\|u\|_{H^{\alpha_{2}}}\prod_{i=3}^{j}\|u\|_{W^{\alpha_{i},\infty}}\\
&\quad\leq C_{\delta}\sum_{j=3}^{2m+2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{j}=0\\
\alpha_{1\cdots j}=2m+2-j\end{subarray}}^{m-1}\|u\|_{H^{\alpha_{1}}}\|u\|_{H^{\alpha_{2}}}\prod_{i=3}^{j}\|u\|_{W^{\alpha_{i},\infty}},\end{split}

where
C_{\delta}
is independent of
2\leq\delta\leq\infty

in view of (
2.10
).
Since
\alpha_{i}\leq m-1\leq\frac{k-1}{2}-1
, we have

(5.35)

\displaystyle\|u\|_{W^{\alpha_{i},\infty}}\lesssim\|u\|_{W^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon,\infty}}.

On the other hand, by setting

(5.36)

\displaystyle\gamma=\frac{2}{k}\bigg(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\sum_{i=3}^{j}\Big(\alpha_{i}+\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon\Big)\bigg)\leq\frac{2}{k}\bigg(2m+1-\frac{1}{2}j+(j-2)\varepsilon\bigg)<2,

it follows from Sobolev’s inequality and (
2.3
) that

(5.37)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|u\|_{H^{\alpha_{1}}}\|u\|_{H^{\alpha_{2}}}\prod_{i=3}^{j}\|u\|_{W^{\alpha_{i},\infty}}&\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{\alpha_{1}}}\|u\|_{H^{\alpha_{2}}}\prod_{i=3}^{j}\|u\|_{H^{\alpha_{i}+\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}}\\
&\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{j-\gamma}\|u\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{\gamma}.\end{split}

Hence, the bound (
5.23
) in this case follows from substituting

u=u_{1}+u_{2}
into
the left-hand side of (
5.34
)
and applying (
5.35
), (
5.37
), and Young’s inequality
(since
\gamma<2
).

\bullet

Case 2:

\ell=2m+1
for
1\leq m\leq\frac{k-2}{2}
.

Proceeding as in (
5.34
),
we can bound the contribution
from (
3.64
) by

(5.38)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{j=3}^{2m+3}\sum_{\#\partial_{x}=0}^{2m+3-j}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p(u)\in\mathcal{P}_{j}(u)\\
\widetilde{p}(u)=\prod_{i=1}^{j}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{i}}u\\
\#\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}(p)=2m+3-j-\#\partial_{x}\\
0\leq\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{j}\leq m\end{subarray}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{T}}p(u)dx\bigg|\\
&\quad\leq C_{\delta}\sum_{j=3}^{2m+3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{j}=0\\
\alpha_{1\cdots j}=2m+3-j\end{subarray}}^{m}\|u\|_{H^{\alpha_{1}}}\|u\|_{H^{\alpha_{2}}}\prod_{i=3}^{j}\|u\|_{W^{\alpha_{i},\infty}},\end{split}

where
C_{\delta}
is independent of
2\leq\delta\leq\infty
.
Since
\alpha_{i}\leq m\leq\frac{k-2}{2}
, (
5.35
) still holds in this case.
By noting that

(5.39)

\displaystyle\gamma=\frac{2}{k}\bigg(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\sum_{i=3}^{j}\Big(\alpha_{i}+\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon\Big)\bigg)\leq\frac{2}{k}\bigg(2m+2-\frac{1}{2}j+(j-2)\varepsilon\bigg)<2

in this case,
the bound (
5.37
) holds with
\gamma
in (
5.39
).
Hence, the bound (
5.23
) in this case also follows from substituting

u=u_{1}+u_{2}
into
the left-hand side of (
5.38
)
and applying (
5.35
), (
5.37
), and Young’s inequality.
∎

We now present a proof of
Lemma
5.7
on the case
\ell=k
,

Proof of Lemma
5.7
.

We separately estimate the contribution
in the cases
k=2m
and
k=2m+1
.
In the following, all the estimates hold uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}

and
all the constants are understood to be uniform in
2\leq\delta\leq\infty
.
As explained in the proof of Lemma
5.6
,
for simplicity of the presentation,
we drop
the Hilbert transform
\mathcal{H}

and the perturbation operator
\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
,
appearing in a monomial
p(u)\in\mathcal{P}_{j}(u)
.
We recall that

X^{\delta}_{N}
and
\Theta_{N}
in (
5.21
) have mean zero on
\mathbb{T}
.

\bullet

Case 1:

k=2m
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

Any monomial
p(u)
in the second term on the right-hand side of (
3.63
)
satisfies
|p(u)|\leq m-1
.
Thus,
we can estimate
the contribution from the second term on the right-hand side of (
3.63
)
deterministically as in Case 1 of the proof of Lemma
5.6
;
see (
5.34
)-(
5.37
).
In particular, note that (
5.35
) and (
5.36
) hold even when
m=\frac{k}{2}

(with
j\geq 3
).
Then, by taking an expectation and applying
Proposition
5.1
(i),
we obtain (
5.24
).

We now consider the first term on the right-hand side of
(
3.63
).
By dropping
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}

(as explained in Case 1 of the proof of Lemma
5.6
),
it suffices to estimate
p(u)=u\partial_{x}^{m-1}u\partial_{x}^{m}u
.
Then, we have

(5.40)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{T}}p(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})dx\bigg]\\
&\quad=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{T}}X^{\delta}_{N}\partial_{x}^{m-1}X^{\delta}_{N}\partial_{x}^{m}X^{\delta}_{N}+(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\partial_{x}^{m-1}(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\partial_{x}^{m}\Theta_{N}\\
&\hphantom{XXXX}+(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\partial_{x}^{m-1}\Theta_{N}\partial_{x}^{m}X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N}\partial_{x}^{m-1}X^{\delta}_{N}\partial_{x}^{m}X^{\delta}_{N}dx\bigg].\end{split}

Noting that
\mathbb{E}[g_{n_{1}}g_{n_{2}}g_{n_{3}}]=0

for any
n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}
,
it follows from (
1.26
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{T}}X^{\delta}_{N}\partial_{x}^{m-1}X^{\delta}_{N}\partial_{x}^{m}X^{\delta}_{N}dx\bigg]=0.

As for the second term on the right-hand side of (
5.40
),
it follows from Hölder’s inequality,
Sobolev’s inequality,
and (
2.3
) that

\displaystyle\bigg|

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\partial_{x}^{m-1}(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\partial_{x}^{m}\Theta_{N}dx\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\|X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-2}{2}}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}

(5.41)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}}}\Big)\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-2}{2}}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-2}{2}}}\Big)\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{k-1-\varepsilon}{k}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{\frac{1+\varepsilon}{k}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-2}{2}}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2}{k}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{\frac{k-2}{k}}\Big)\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}.

By noting that
\frac{1+\varepsilon}{k}+\frac{k-2}{k}+1<2

and applying Young’s inequality and
Proposition
5.1
(i), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\eqref{DDV13}\big]\leq C+\mathbb{E}\Big[C_{\varepsilon_{0}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}+\varepsilon_{0}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{2}\Big]

for some
\alpha\gg 1
.
As for the third term on the right-hand side of (
5.40
),
integrating by parts, we have

(5.42)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\partial_{x}^{m-1}\Theta_{N}\partial_{x}^{m}X^{\delta}_{N}dx&=-\int_{\mathbb{T}}\partial_{x}(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\partial_{x}^{m-1}\Theta_{N}\partial_{x}^{m-1}X^{\delta}_{N}dx\\
&\quad-\int_{\mathbb{T}}(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\partial_{x}^{m}\Theta_{N}\partial_{x}^{m-1}X^{\delta}_{N}dx,\end{split}

where the first term on the right-hand side
satisfies
|p(u)|\leq m-1

and thus can be
handled
in the same manner as the second term on the right-hand side of (
3.63
)
discussed above,
while the second term on the right-hand side of (
5.42
) was already treated in (
5.41
).
Finally, as for the
last term on the right-hand side of (
5.40
),
by Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality
(recall that
\Theta_{N}
has mean zero on
\mathbb{T}
)
and Cauchy’s inequality, we have
30
30

30

Here,
we crucially use the fact that the Hilbert transform (if any) does not act on multiple factors but
acts only on each factor; see Lemma
3.9
.

(5.43)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Theta_{N}\partial_{x}^{m-1}X^{\delta}_{N}\partial_{x}^{m}X^{\delta}_{N}dx\bigg|&\leq\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{m}}\big\|\mathbf{P}_{\neq 0}(\partial_{x}^{m-1}X^{\delta}_{N}\partial_{x}^{m}X^{\delta}_{N})\big\|_{H^{-m}}\\
&\leq\varepsilon_{0}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{2}+C_{\varepsilon_{0}}\big\|\mathbf{P}_{\neq 0}(\partial_{x}^{\frac{k}{2}-1}X^{\delta}_{N}\partial_{x}^{\frac{k}{2}}X^{\delta}_{N})\big\|_{H^{-\frac{k}{2}}}^{2}.\end{split}

Then,
from Lemma
5.9

(see also Remark
5.10
),
we obtain

(5.44)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\eqref{DDV16}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{0}\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\Theta_{N}\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}\Big]+C_{\varepsilon_{0}}.

Putting everything together, we conclude that
(
5.24
) holds in this case.

\bullet

Case 2:

k=2m+1
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

Any monomial
p(u)
in the last term on the right-hand side of (
3.64
)
satisfies
|p(u)|\leq m-1=\frac{k-3}{2}
,
and thus we can estimate
the last term on the right-hand side of (
3.64
)
deterministically as in Case 2 of the proof of Lemma
5.6
.
Then, by taking an expectation and applying Proposition
5.1
(i),
we obtain (
5.24
).

We now consider the
first term on the right-hand side of (
3.64
).
As before, it suffices to consider
p(u)=u\partial_{x}^{m}u\partial_{x}^{m}u
.
Then, we have

(5.45)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{T}}p(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})dx\bigg]&=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{T}}X^{\delta}_{N}(\partial_{x}^{m}X^{\delta}_{N})^{2}+\Theta_{N}(\partial_{x}^{m}X^{\delta}_{N})^{2}\\
&\hphantom{XXX}+(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\partial_{x}^{m}\Theta_{N}\partial_{x}^{m}(2X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})dx\bigg].\end{split}

Noting that
\mathbb{E}[g_{n_{1}}g_{n_{2}}g_{n_{3}}]=0

for any
n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}
,
it follows from (
1.26
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{T}}X^{\delta}_{N}(\partial_{x}^{m}X^{\delta}_{N})^{2}dx\bigg]=0.

From
Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality
(recall that
\Theta_{N}
has mean zero on
\mathbb{T}
)
and Cauchy’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Theta_{N}(\partial_{x}^{m}X^{\delta}_{N})^{2}dx\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{m+\frac{1}{2}}}\big\|\mathbf{P}_{\neq 0}((\partial_{x}^{m}X^{\delta}_{N})^{2})\big\|_{H^{-m-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{0}\|\Theta_{N}\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}+C_{\varepsilon_{0}}\big\|\mathbf{P}_{\neq 0}((\partial_{x}^{\frac{k-1}{2}}X^{\delta}_{N})^{2})\big\|_{H^{-\frac{k}{2}}}^{2}.

Then,
from Lemma
5.9

(see also Remark
5.10
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Theta_{N}(\partial_{x}^{m}X^{\delta}_{N})^{2}dx\bigg]\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{0}\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\Theta_{N}\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}\Big]+C_{\varepsilon_{0}}.

As for the last term on the right-hand side of (
5.45
),
from (fractional) integration by parts, Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality, the fractional Leibniz rule (
2.4
),
(
2.3
), and Sobolev’s inequality, we have

(5.46)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bigg|&\int_{\mathbb{T}}(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\partial_{x}^{m}\Theta_{N}\partial_{x}^{m}(2X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})dx\bigg|\\
&\lesssim\|(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\partial_{x}^{\frac{k-1}{2}}\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\frac{1}{4}}}\Big)\\
&\lesssim\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{W^{\frac{1}{4},\infty}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{3+\varepsilon}{4}}}\Big)\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{2k-1}{4}}}\\
&\quad\times\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{2k}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{\frac{2k-3}{2k}}\Big)\\
&\lesssim\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{W^{\frac{1}{4},\infty}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2k-3-\varepsilon}{2k}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{\frac{3+\varepsilon}{2k}}\Big)\\
&\quad\times\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2k}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{\frac{2k-1}{2k}}\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{2k}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{\frac{2k-3}{2k}}\Big).\end{split}

By noting that

\frac{3+\varepsilon}{2k}+\frac{2k-1}{2k}+\frac{2k-3}{2k}<2

and applying Young’s inequality and
Proposition
5.1
(i),
we obtain

(5.47)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\eqref{DDV5}\big]\leq C_{\varepsilon_{0}}+\mathbb{E}\Big[C_{\varepsilon_{0}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}+\varepsilon_{0}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{2}\Big]

for some
\alpha\gg 1
.

Next, we consider the
second term on the right-hand side of (
3.64
).
In this case, it suffices to consider

\widetilde{p}(u)=\partial_{x}^{\kappa}u\partial_{x}^{m-1}u\partial_{x}^{m}u

with
\#\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}=1-\kappa
,
k=0,1
.
Moreover, we may assume
m=\frac{k-1}{2}\geq 2
since
the case
m=1
reduces to

p(u)=u\partial_{x}^{m}u\partial_{x}^{m}u
(or even easier
p(u)=u^{2}\partial_{x}u
)
which we handled above.
Proceeding as in (
5.46
) with
Lemma
2.4
(iii), we can bound the contribution in this case by

\displaystyle\bigg|

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\partial_{x}^{m-1}(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\partial_{x}^{m}(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})dx\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta}\|\partial_{x}^{\kappa}(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\partial_{x}^{\frac{k-3}{2}}(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\frac{1}{4}}}\Big)

(5.48)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta}\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{W^{\frac{5}{4},\infty}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{7+\varepsilon}{4}}}\Big)\|(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\|_{H^{\frac{2k-5}{4}}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{2k}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{\frac{2k-3}{2k}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{W^{\frac{5}{4},\infty}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2k-7-\varepsilon}{2k}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{\frac{7+\varepsilon}{2k}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{5}{2k}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{\frac{2k-5}{2k}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{2k}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{\frac{2k-3}{2k}}\Big).

By noting that

\frac{7+\varepsilon}{2k}+\frac{2k-5}{2k}+\frac{2k-3}{2k}<2

and applying Young’s inequality and
Proposition
5.1
(i), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\eqref{DDV7}\big]\leq C_{\delta,\varepsilon_{0}}+\mathbb{E}\Big[C_{\delta,\varepsilon_{0}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}+\varepsilon_{0}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{2}\Big]

for some
\alpha\gg 1
,
where the constant
C_{\delta,\varepsilon_{0}}>0
is independent of
2\leq\delta\leq\infty
.

Lastly, we consider the
third term on the right-hand side of (
3.64
).
In this case, it suffices to consider

p(u)=u^{2}\partial_{x}^{m-1}u\partial_{x}^{m}u
.
Then, proceeding as in (
5.46
), we have
31
31

31

Here,
if there is the Hilbert transform
\mathcal{H}
acting on
multiple factors including the last factor

\partial_{x}^{m}(2X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})
,
then by the anti self-adjointness of
\mathcal{H}
,
we move the action of
\mathcal{H}
onto the remaining factors
(which in particular does not include the last factor

\partial_{x}^{m}(2X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})
such that we can apply Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality
to separate the last factor as in the first step of (
5.49
).

\displaystyle\bigg|

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})^{2}\partial_{x}^{m-1}(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\partial_{x}^{m}(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})dx\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})^{2}\partial_{x}^{\frac{k-3}{2}}(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\frac{1}{4}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{W^{\frac{1}{4},\infty}}^{2}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{3+\varepsilon}{4}}}^{2}\Big)\|(X^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\|_{H^{\frac{2k-5}{4}}}

(5.49)

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{2k}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{\frac{2k-3}{2k}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{W^{\frac{1}{4},\infty}}^{2}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2k-3-\varepsilon}{k}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{\frac{3+\varepsilon}{k}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{5}{2k}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{\frac{2k-5}{2k}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{2k}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{\frac{2k-3}{2k}}\Big).

By noting that

\frac{3+\varepsilon}{k}+\frac{2k-5}{2k}+\frac{2k-3}{2k}<2

and applying Young’s inequality and
Proposition
5.1
(i), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\eqref{DDV9}\big]\leq C_{\varepsilon_{0}}+\mathbb{E}\Big[C_{\varepsilon_{0}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}+\varepsilon_{0}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{2}\Big]

for some
\alpha\gg 1
.

This concludes the proof of Lemma
5.7
.
∎

5.3.
Construction of the deep-water generalized Gibbs measures

In this subsection, we present a proof of
Theorem
1.6
(i).
We first state a lemma
on
uniform (in
N
and
\delta
)
convergence of the truncated interaction potential

R^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})
,
where
R^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)

and
X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
are as in
(
3.62
)
and (
1.26
), respectively.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
k\geq 2
be an integer.
Given any
0<\delta\leq\infty

and finite
p\geq 1
,
the sequence
\{R^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}

converges
to
R^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})

in
L^{p}(\Omega)
as
N\to\infty
.
Moreover, given any finite
p\geq 1
,
there exist
C_{\delta}=C_{\delta}(p)>0
and
\theta>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{2\leq\delta\leq\infty}\|R^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\delta}<\infty,

\displaystyle\|R^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{M}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})-R^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\delta}}{N^{\theta}}

for any
M\geq N\geq 1
,
where the constant
C_{\delta}
is independent of
2\leq\delta\leq\infty
.
In particular, the rate of convergence is uniform in
2\leq\delta\leq\infty
.

Before proceeding to a proof of Lemma
5.11
,
we present a sketch of a proof of Theorem
1.6
(i).

Proof of Theorem
1.6
(i).

Given finite
p\geq 1
,
the claimed
L^{p}(\Omega)
-convergence (
1.31
) of the truncated density

F^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})
, which is uniform in
2\leq\delta\leq\infty
, follows
from

(i)

the uniform (in
N\in\mathbb{N}
)
L^{q}(\Omega)
-bounds, for each
0<\delta\leq\infty
, on
the truncated density

F^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})
for some
q>p

(Proposition
5.3
),
which is also uniform
in
2\leq\delta\leq\infty
,

(ii)

the convergence in probability, for each
0<\delta\leq\infty
, of

R^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})
(Lemma
5.11
)
and

\|\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

(Proposition
5.1
(i)),
which is uniform
in
2\leq\delta\leq\infty
,

(iii)

the uniform continuous mapping theorem
(Lemma 2.9 in
[
64
]
)
which implies uniform (in
2\leq\delta\leq\infty
)
convergence in probability of
the truncated density

F^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)
for some
q>p
.

See Step 2 in the proof of
[
64
, Proposition 3.6]

for further details.
See also

[
109
, Remark 3.8]

and the end of the proof of
[
91
, Proposition 1.2]
.

∎

Remark 5.12
.

As mentioned in Remark
1.11
,
it is also possible to work with
a sharp
L^{2}
-cutoff
\mathbf{1}_{\{\|u\|_{L^{2}}\leq K\}}

in place of the smooth
L^{2}
-cutoff

\eta_{K}\big(\|u\|_{L^{2}}\big)
.
In this case, from the almost sure convergence of

\|\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

which is uniform in
2\leq\delta\leq\infty

(Proposition
5.1
(i))
and the fact that

(5.50)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}=K\Big)=0

for any
0<\delta\leq\infty
and
K>0
,
we obtain almost sure convergence of

\mathbf{1}_{\{\|\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\leq K\}}
,
(and hence convergence in
L^{q}(\Omega)
,
1\leq q<\infty
, via the bounded convergence theorem)
which is uniform in
2\leq\delta\leq\infty
,
and the rest follows as above.
As for (
5.50
), see Lemma 2.4 in
[
89
]

(in a more complicated situation of the renormalized
L^{2}
-norm).

Before presenting a proof of
Lemma
5.11
, let us introduce a definition.

Definition 5.13
.

Given
\{n_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\ell}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{*}
,
we say that we have a

pair
, if
there exist distinct

j_{1},j_{2}\in\{1,\dots,\ell\}
such that

n_{j_{1}}+n_{j_{2}}=0
.
For example,

(i)

when
\ell=3
, there is no pair
under
n_{123}=0
, since
n_{j}\neq 0
,
j=1,2,3
,

(ii)

when
\ell=4
, we have either no pair or two pairs.

We now present a proof of
Lemma
5.11
.

Proof of Lemma
5.11
.

Fix an integer
k\geq 2
and finite
p\geq 1
.
Let
A_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{\ell}{2}}
be as in (
3.63
), (
3.64
), and (
3.65
).
Then, in view of (
3.62
),
it suffices to prove that there exist

C_{\delta}=C_{\delta}(p)>0

and
\theta>0

such that

\displaystyle\|A_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{\ell}{2}}(X^{\delta}_{M})-A_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{\ell}{2}}(X^{\delta}_{N})\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\delta}}{N^{\theta}},

for any
M\geq N\geq 1
and
1\leq\ell\leq k
,
where the constant
C_{\delta}
is independent of
2\leq\delta\leq\infty

and
1\leq\ell\leq k
.
Here, we used the notation (
5.21
).
In the following, all the implicit constants are independent of
N,M\in\mathbb{N}
and
2\leq\delta\leq\infty
.
In view of the Wiener chaos estimate (Lemma
2.8
),
we only consider the case
p=2
.

We first consider the case
\ell=1
.
By
Proposition
5.1
(i), we have

\displaystyle\|A_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{1}{2}}(X^{\delta}_{M})-A_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{1}{2}}(X^{\delta}_{N})\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|X^{\delta}_{M}-X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{L^{6}_{\omega}L^{3}_{x}}\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{M}\|^{2}_{L^{6}_{\omega}L^{3}_{x}}+\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|^{2}_{L^{6}_{\omega}L^{3}_{x}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta}\frac{1}{N^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon}}.

\bullet

Case 1:

\ell=2m
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
with
m\leq\frac{k}{2}
.

Denote the
j
th term on the right-hand side of (
3.63
) by
A^{(j)}_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{\ell}{2}}
,

j=1,2
.
Then, we have

(5.51)

\displaystyle\begin{split}A_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{\ell}{2}}(X^{\delta}_{M})-A_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{\ell}{2}}(X^{\delta}_{N})&=\Big(A^{(1)}_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{\ell}{2}}(X^{\delta}_{M})-A^{(1)}_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{\ell}{2}}(X^{\delta}_{N})\Big)\\
&\quad+\Big(A^{(2)}_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{\ell}{2}}(X^{\delta}_{M})-A^{(2)}_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{\ell}{2}}(X^{\delta}_{N})\Big)\\
&=:\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 1.42262pt+\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}.\end{split}

We first estimate
I
.
Without loss of generality,
we assume that

p(u)=u\partial_{x}^{m-1}u\partial_{x}^{m}u
. Then, from (
1.26
), we have

(5.52)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 1.42262pt\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}&=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{n_{123}=0}\Big(\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|n_{j}|\leq M\\
j=1,2,3\end{subarray}}-\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|n_{j}|\leq N\\
j=1,2,3\end{subarray}}\Big)\frac{g_{n_{1}}g_{n_{2}}g_{n_{3}}(in_{2})^{m-1}(in_{3})^{m}}{(T_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(n_{1})T_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(n_{2})T_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(n_{3}))^{\frac{1}{2}}}\\
&\hphantom{XXXX}\times\sum_{\widetilde{n}_{123}=0}\Big(\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|\widetilde{n}_{j}|\leq M\\
j=1,2,3\end{subarray}}-\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|\widetilde{n}_{j}|\leq N\\
j=1,2,3\end{subarray}}\Big)\frac{\overline{g_{\widetilde{n}_{1}}g_{\widetilde{n}_{2}}g_{\widetilde{n}_{3}}}(i\widetilde{n}_{2})^{m-1}(i\widetilde{n}_{3})^{m}}{(T_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(\widetilde{n}_{1})T_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(\widetilde{n}_{2})T_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(\widetilde{n}_{3}))^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg].\end{split}

Note that, in the sums above, there is no pair in
(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})

and in
(\widetilde{n}_{1},\widetilde{n}_{2},\widetilde{n}_{3})

in the sense of Definition
5.13
.
32
32

32

In particular,

I
belongs to the homogeneous Wiener chaoses of order
3
.

Thus,
in order to compute the expectation above,
we need to take all possible pairings between
(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})

and
(\widetilde{n}_{1},\widetilde{n}_{2},\widetilde{n}_{3})
.
By Jensen’s inequality, however,
we see that it suffices to consider the case

n_{j}=\widetilde{n}_{j}
,
j=1,2,3
.
See the discussion on
in Section 4 of
[
75
]
.
See also Section 10 in
[
44
]

and the proof of Lemma B.1 in
[
83
]
.

As a result,
by applying (
5.1
)
and noting that
\max(|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|)\gtrsim N
in (
5.52
) (which follows from

\max_{j=1,2,3}|n_{j}|>N
and the triangle inequality),
we have

(5.53)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 1.42262pt\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}&\lesssim_{\delta}\sum_{n_{123}=0}\Big(\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|n_{j}|\leq M\\
j=1,2,3\end{subarray}}-\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|n_{j}|\leq N\\
j=1,2,3\end{subarray}}\Big)^{2}\frac{1}{\langle n_{1}\rangle^{k}\langle n_{2}\rangle^{k-2m+2}\langle n_{3}\rangle^{k-2m}}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\\
\max(|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|)\gtrsim N\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\langle n_{1}\rangle^{k}\langle n_{2}\rangle^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{N},\end{split}

where, in the last step, we used the fact that

k\geq 2
.

Next, we consider

I I
in (
5.51
).
From the definition of

A^{(2)}_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{\ell}{2}}
,
we have
|p(u)|\leq m-1\leq\frac{k}{2}-1
.
Then, from
multilinearity of the monomials,
(
5.34
), (
5.35
),

(5.54)

\displaystyle\|u\|_{H^{\alpha_{i}}}\leq\|u\|_{H^{m-1}}\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon-(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon)}}

for
i=1,2
,
and Proposition
5.1
(i),
we have

\displaystyle\|\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}\Big(1+\|X^{\delta}_{M}\|_{L^{4m+4}_{\omega}W^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon,\infty}}^{2m+1}+\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{L^{4m+4}_{\omega}W^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon,\infty}}^{2m+1}\Big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{Xl}\times\|X^{\delta}_{M}-X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{L^{4m+4}_{\omega}W^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}.

\bullet

Case 2:

\ell=2m+1\geq 3
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
with
m\leq\frac{k-1}{2}
.

In this case, we have
k\geq 3
.
In view of (
3.64
), we write

\displaystyle A^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{2m+1}{2}}(X^{\delta}_{M})-A^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{2m+1}{2}}(X^{\delta}_{N})

\displaystyle=:\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I \hskip-7.96677pt I}+\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt V}+\text{V}+\text{V \hskip-7.96677pt I},

where

I I I
,
I V
,
V
, and
V I
correspond to the contributions
from the first, second, third, and fourth terms on the right-hand side of (
3.64
).

We first estimate
I I I
and
I V
when
k\geq 3
.
Proceeding as in (
5.52
) and (
5.53
)
with Lemma
2.7
, we have

\displaystyle\|\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I \hskip-7.96677pt I}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta}\sum_{n_{123}=0}\Big(\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|n_{j}|\leq M\\
j=1,2,3\end{subarray}}-\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|n_{j}|\leq N\\
j=1,2,3\end{subarray}}\Big)^{2}\frac{1}{\langle n_{1}\rangle^{k}\langle n_{2}\rangle^{k-2m}\langle n_{3}\rangle^{k-2m}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{123}=0\\
\max(|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|)\gtrsim N\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\langle n_{1}\rangle^{k}\langle n_{2}\rangle\langle n_{3}\rangle}\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{1-\varepsilon}}.

Similarly, with (
2.10
)
and
k\geq 3
, we have

\displaystyle\|\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt V}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta}\sum_{\kappa=0}^{1}\frac{1}{\delta^{1-\kappa}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{123}=0\\
\max(|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|)\gtrsim N\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\langle n_{1}\rangle^{k-2\kappa}\langle n_{2}\rangle^{k-2m+2}\langle n_{3}\rangle^{k-2m}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta}\frac{1}{N^{1-\varepsilon}},

where we used the fact that
k-2\kappa\geq 1
.
In handling
I I I
and
I V
,
we used the fact that there is no pair in
(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})
.

Let us now consider
V
coming form the third term on the right-hand side of (
3.64
).
Without loss of generality,
we assume that

p(u)=u^{2}\partial_{x}^{m-1}u\partial_{x}^{m}u
. Then, from (
1.26
), we have

(5.55)

\displaystyle\text{V}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\sum_{n_{1234}=0}\Big(\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|n_{j}|\leq M\\
j=1,\dots,4\end{subarray}}-\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|n_{j}|\leq N\\
j=1,\dots,4\end{subarray}}\Big)\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{4}g_{n_{j}}(in_{3})^{m-1}(in_{4})^{m}}{\prod_{j=1}^{4}(T_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(n_{j}))^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Since it is quartic,
we have either no pair or two pairs
in the sense of Definition
5.13
.
Write
\text{V}=\text{V}_{1}+\text{V}_{2}
,
where
\text{V}_{1}
denotes the contribution from frequencies with no pair
in
(

),
while

\text{V}_{2}
denotes the contribution from frequencies with two pairs in
(
5.55
).
Then, proceeding with Jensen’s inequality as in (

)-(
5.52
)
with (
5.1
),
we have

\displaystyle\|\text{V}_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1234}=0\\
\max(|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|,|n_{3}|)\gtrsim N\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\langle n_{1}\rangle^{k}\langle n_{2}\rangle^{k}\langle n_{3}\rangle^{3}\langle n_{4}\rangle}\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2}}.

On the other hand, when there are two pairs in (
5.55
),
by Minkowski’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|\text{V}_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
\max(|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|)\gtrsim N\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\langle n_{1}\rangle^{2}\langle n_{2}\rangle^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{N}.

Lastly, we bound
V I

corresponding to the last term on the right-hand side of (

),
where
we have
|p(u)|\leq m-1\leq\frac{k-1}{2}-1
.
Then,

with

)
and Proposition

(i),
we have

\displaystyle\|\text{V \hskip-7.96677pt I}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\quad\leq N^{-1+\varepsilon}\Big(\|X^{\delta}_{M}\|_{L^{4m+6}_{\omega}W^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon,\infty}}^{2m+2}+\|X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{L^{4m+6}_{\omega}W^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon,\infty}}^{2m+2}\Big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{XXl}\times\|X^{\delta}_{M}-X^{\delta}_{N}\|_{L^{4m+6}_{\omega}W^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon,\infty}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim N^{-1+\varepsilon}.

This concludes

.
∎

5.4.
Deep-water convergence of the generalized Gibbs measures

In this subsection, we present a proof of Theorem
1.6
(ii).
We first state a preliminary result
on the
L^{p}(\Omega)
-convergence properties of the truncated density

F^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)
defined in (
5.13
).

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
k\geq 2
be an integer and
K>0
.
Then, given any finite
p\geq 1
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

(5.56)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to\infty}\|F^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})-F^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}})\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=0,

(5.57)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to\infty}\|F^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}})-F^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}})\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=0.

In order to prove Lemma
5.14
,
we need the following auxiliary lemma
on
an
N
-dependent
(but uniform in
2\leq\delta\leq\infty
)
deterministic bound
on the truncated density
F^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)
.

Lemma 5.15
.

Let
k\geq 2
be an integer and
K>0
.
Then, given any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
there exists a finite constant
C_{k,K,N}>0
such that

(5.58)

\displaystyle\sup_{2\leq\delta\leq\infty}\big|F^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\big|\leq C_{k,K,N}<\infty

for any
u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T})
.

Proof.

From (
5.13
),
we have

\displaystyle F^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)=\eta_{K}\big(\|\mathbf{P}_{N}u\|_{L^{2}}\big)\exp\Big(-R^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\Big),

where
R^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)
is as in (
3.62
).
Recall from (
3.62
), (
3.63
), (
3.64
), and (
3.65
)
that
R^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)

is a polynomial in
u
with derivatives, non-positive powers of
\delta
,

\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
, and the Hilbert transform.
Then,
the bound (
5.58
) follows from
Bernstein’s inequality (
\|\mathbf{P}_{N}u\|_{W^{s,\infty}}\lesssim N^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}u\|_{L^{2}}

for any
s\geq 0
),
Lemma
2.4
, and the fact that
\|\mathbf{P}_{N}u\|_{L^{2}}\leq 2K
.
∎

We now present a proof of Lemma
5.14
.

Proof of Lemma
5.14
.

We first prove (
5.57
).
From (the proof of) (
3.43
) in Proposition
3.8
,
we have

(5.59)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to\infty}R^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)=R^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)

for any

u\in H^{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbb{T})
,
where

R^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)=R^{\text{\rm BO}}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)
is as in (
1.12
).
Then,
from (
5.13
) and (
5.59
),
we obtain

(5.60)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to\infty}F^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\omega))=F^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\omega))

for each
\omega\in\Omega
.
Therefore, (
5.57
) follows from (
5.60
),
Lemma
5.15
, and the bounded convergence theorem.

Next, we prove (
5.56
).
For each fixed
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
it follows from (
1.26
) and (
5.1
)
that

\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}

converges to
\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}

in
H^{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbb{T})
for any
\omega\in\Omega
.
Thus, we have

(5.61)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to\infty}\eta_{K}\big(\|\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\omega)\|_{L^{2}}\big)=\eta_{K}\big(\|\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\omega)\|_{L^{2}}\big)

for any
\omega\in\Omega
.
We claim that

(5.62)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{\delta\to\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\{\|\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\omega)\|_{L^{2}}\leq 2K\}}R^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\omega))\\
&\quad=\mathbf{1}_{\{\|\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\omega)\|_{L^{2}}\leq 2K\}}R^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\omega)).\end{split}

For now, let us assume (
5.62
) and prove (
5.56
).
From (
5.13
), (
5.61
), and (
5.62
),
we have

(5.63)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to\infty}F^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\omega))=F^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\omega))

for any
\omega\in\Omega
.
Then, (
5.56
) follows from (
5.63
),
Lemma
5.15
, and the bounded convergence theorem.

It remains to prove (
5.62
).
Recall the definition (
3.62
) of
R^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)
.
As for the second term on the right-hand side of (
3.62
),
arguing as in the proof of Lemma
5.15
, we have

(5.64)

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in L^{2}}\bigg|\mathbf{1}_{\{\|\mathbf{P}_{N}u\|_{L^{2}}\leq 2K\}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1}\frac{1}{\delta^{\ell}}A_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{k-\ell}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\bigg|\lesssim\frac{C_{N}}{\delta}\longrightarrow 0,

as
\delta\to\infty
.
Let

D^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)

be the collection of monomials
p(u)
in
A^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{k}{2}}(u)

such that
\#\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}(p)\geq 1
.
In view of
(
2.10
),
we have

(5.65)

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in L^{2}}\bigg|\mathbf{1}_{\{\||\mathbf{P}_{N}u\|_{L^{2}}\leq 2K\}}D^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\bigg|\lesssim\frac{C_{N}}{\delta}\longrightarrow 0,

as
\delta\to\infty
.

Let
G^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)=A^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2},\frac{k}{2}}(u)-D^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)
.
Then, by noting that
G^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
is
\delta
-free
in the sense of the proof of Proposition
3.8
,
we see that
G^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)
is independent of
\delta

and that
G^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)=R^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)
.
See also (
3.44
) and
(
3.46
).
Hence, from the
H^{\frac{k}{2}}
-convergence of

\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\omega)

to
\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\omega)
,
we obtain

(5.66)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to\infty}G^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\omega))=\lim_{\delta\to\infty}R^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\omega))=R^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}X^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\omega))

for any
\omega\in\Omega
.
Hence, the claim (
5.62
) follows from (
5.64
), (
5.65
), and (
5.66
) with (
3.62
).
∎

We conclude this section by presenting a proof of Theorem
1.6
(ii).

Proof of Theorem
1.6
(ii).

The claimed equivalence
of
\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
and

\rho^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}

follows from
(a)
the equivalence
of
\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
and
the Gaussian measure with the
L^{2}
-cutoff

\eta_{K}\big(\|u\|_{L^{2}}\big)d\mu^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)

for any
0<\delta\leq\infty
,
and
(b) Proposition
5.1
(iii).

In the following,
by closely following the argument in
Subsection 3.3 of
[
64
]
,
we prove
convergence in total variation of

\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
to
\rho^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}
.
Given any
N\in\mathbb{N}
, from the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle d_{\rm TV}\big(\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}},\rho^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq d_{\rm TV}\big(\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}},\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2},N}\big)+d_{\rm TV}\big(\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2},N},\rho^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2},N}\big)+d_{\rm TV}\big(\rho^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2},N},\rho^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{2\leq\delta\leq\infty}d_{\rm TV}\big(\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}},\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2},N}\big)+d_{\rm TV}\big(\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2},N},\rho^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2},N}\big)

for any
2\leq\delta<\infty
.
In view of the uniform (in
2\leq\delta\leq\infty
)
convergence in total variation of
\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2},N}
to
\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}

(Theorem
1.6
(i)),
by taking
a limit as
\delta\to\infty
and then a limit as
N\to\infty
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to\infty}d_{\rm TV}\big(\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}},\rho^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}\big)\leq\lim_{N\to\infty}\lim_{\delta\to\infty}d_{\rm TV}\big(\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2},N},\rho^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2},N}\big).

Hence, it suffices to prove

(5.67)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to\infty}d_{\rm TV}\big(\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2},N},\rho^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2},N}\big)=0

for some
N\in\mathbb{N}
(but we will prove (
5.67
) for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
).

Recall from (
5.56
) in Lemma
5.14
that
the partition function

Z_{\delta,\frac{k}{2},N}=\|F^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\|_{L^{1}(d\mu^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})}

of the truncated ILW generalized Gibbs measure
\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2},N}

converges
to the partition function

Z_{\infty,\frac{k}{2},N}=\|F^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\|_{L^{1}(d\mu^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}})}

of the truncated BO generalized Gibbs measure
\rho^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2},N}
.
Then,
proceeding as in
[
64
, (3.57)]
,
with
(
2.25
), we have

(5.68)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{\delta\to\infty}d_{\rm TV}\big(\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2},N},\rho^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2},N}\big)=\lim_{\delta\to\infty}\sup_{A\in\mathcal{B}}\big|\rho^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2},N}(A)-\rho^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2},N}(A)\big|\\
&\quad\leq Z_{\infty,\frac{k}{2},N}^{-1}\lim_{\delta\to\infty}\int_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon}}|F^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)-F^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)|\,d\mu^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)\\
&\quad\quad+Z_{\infty,\frac{k}{2},N}^{-1}\lim_{\delta\to\infty}\int_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon}}F^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\bigg|\frac{d\mu^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}}{d\mu^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}}(u)-1\bigg|\,d\mu^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)\\
&\quad=:\lim_{\delta\to\infty}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 1.42262pt_{\delta,N}+\lim_{\delta\to\infty}\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{\delta,N},\end{split}

where
\mathcal{B}
denote the Borel sets in
H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon}(\mathbb{T})
.
From (
5.57
) in Lemma
5.14
,
we obtain

(5.69)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to\infty}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 1.42262pt_{\delta,N}=0

for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
On the other hand, from Lemma
5.15
,
Scheffé’s theorem
(Lemma 2.1 in
[
58
]
; see also Proposition 1.2.7 in
[
108
]
),
and Proposition
5.1
(iv), we obtain

(5.70)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{\delta\to\infty}\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{\delta,N}&\leq C_{k,K,N}\lim_{\delta\to\infty}\int_{H^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\bigg|\frac{d\mu^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}}{d\mu^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}}(u)-1\bigg|\,d\mu^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}(u)\\
&=2C_{k,K,N}\lim_{\delta\to\infty}d_{\rm TV}\big(\mu^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}},\mu^{\infty}_{\frac{k}{2}}\big)=0\end{split}

for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
Therefore, (
5.67
) follows from (
5.68
), (
5.69
),
and (
5.70
).
∎

6.
Shallow-water generalized Gibbs measures

In this section,
we go over the construction of the generalized Gibbs measures in the shallow-water regime
and study their convergence properties in the shallow-water limit
(Theorem
1.7
(i) and (ii)).
As in the deep-water regime studied in the previous section,
there are two main tasks:

(i)

We need to establish
an

L^{p}
-integrability of
the truncated density
\widetilde{F}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}v)
,
which is
uniform in

both

N\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<\delta\ll 1
.
As in Section
5
,
we employ a variational approach.
Thanks to our detailed analysis
in Section
4

on the structure
of the shallow-water conservation laws,
this part of analysis via the variational formula
follows closely to that in the deep-water regime.
See Subsection
6.2
.

(ii)

We also need to establish weak convergence
of
the base Gaussian measures

\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
in (
1.32
)
(see also (
1.40
)).
See Proposition
6.1
.

Unlike the deep-water case
(Proposition
5.1
),
the convergence of the base Gaussian measures
holds only weakly.
In Proposition
6.1
,
we also establish
singularity of
the base Gaussian measures

\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}

and the limiting
base Gaussian measure

\widetilde{\mu}^{0}_{\lceil\frac{k}{2}\rceil}
,
which also exhibits a sharp contrast
to the deep-water case.
Furthermore,
we establish
an interesting dichotomy
on singularity / equivalence
for the odd-order and
even-order Gaussian measures
with different depth parameters
\delta
;
see Proposition
6.1
.

In Subsection
6.3
,
we present a proof of Theorem
1.7
(i)
on the construction of the shallow-water generalized Gibbs measure for fixed
0\leq\delta<\infty
,
while we present
a proof of Theorem
1.7
(ii) and (iii)
in Subsection
6.4
.

6.1.
Singularity and equivalence of the base Gaussian measures

Given

0\leq\delta<\infty
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
be
the Gaussian measure defined in (
1.32
)
and (
1.40
).
Recall that

\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
is the induced probability measure
under the map
\omega\in\Omega\mapsto\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\omega)
,
where
\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
is as in (
1.33
)
and (
1.41
).

The following proposition generalizes
Proposition 4.1 in
[
64
]

(see also Proposition 4.2 in
[
64
]
),
where
the
k=1
case was treated.
In this proposition,
we in particular establish a 2-to-1 collapse
of the Gaussian measure
\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}

in the shallow-water limit.
Furthermore, we point out that
the Gaussian measure

\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
and its shallow-water limit

\widetilde{\mu}^{0}_{\frac{k}{2}}

are singular
and that
convergence
of
\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}

holds only weakly,
which presents a stark contrast to the deep-water case
(Proposition
5.1
).

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, the following statements hold.

(i)

Given any
0\leq\delta<\infty
,
finite
p\geq 1
, and
1\leq r\leq\infty
,
the sequence
\{\mathbf{P}_{N}\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}

converges
to
\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
in
L^{p}(\Omega;W^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon,r}(\mathbb{T}))

as
N\to\infty
.
Moreover, given any finite
p\geq 1

and
1\leq r\leq\infty
,
there exist
C_{\delta}=C_{\delta}(p,r)>0
and
\theta>0
such that

(6.1)

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{0<\delta\leq 1}\Big\|\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}\|_{W^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon,r}_{x}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle<C_{\delta}<\infty,

(6.2)

\displaystyle\sup_{0<\delta\leq 1}\Big\|\|\mathbf{P}_{M}\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}-\mathbf{P}_{N}\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}\|_{W^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon,r}_{x}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\delta}}{N^{\theta}}

for any
M\geq N\geq 1
,
where the constant
C_{\delta}
is independent of
0<\delta\leq 1
.
In particular, the rate of convergence is uniform in
0<\delta\leq 1
.
When
k
is even, (
6.1
) and (
6.2
) hold
including
\delta=0
.

(ii)

Given any finite
p\geq 1
and
1\leq r\leq\infty
,

\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
converges to

\widetilde{X}^{0}_{\lceil\frac{k}{2}\rceil}
in
L^{p}(\Omega;W^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon,r}(\mathbb{T}))

as
\delta\to 0
,
where
\lceil x\rceil
denotes
the smallest integer greater than or equal to
x
.
In particular,
\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
converges weakly to
\widetilde{\mu}^{0}_{\lceil\frac{k}{2}\rceil}
as
\delta\to\infty
.

(iii)

Let
0<\delta<\infty
.
Then, the Gaussian measures
\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
and

\widetilde{\mu}^{0}_{\lceil\frac{k}{2}\rceil}

are singular.

(iv)

Let
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<\delta_{1}<\delta_{2}<\infty
.
Then, the odd-order Gaussian measures
\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta_{1}}_{\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}
and

\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta_{2}}_{\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}
are singular,
while the even-order Gaussian measures
\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta_{1}}_{\kappa}
and

\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta_{2}}_{\kappa}
are equivalent.

Before proceeding to a proof of Proposition
6.1
, we
state a preliminary lemma on
the multiplier

\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}
defined in (
1.34
).

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<\delta<\infty
.
Then, we have

(6.3)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)=\lim_{\delta\to 0}\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa}(n)=n^{2\kappa}=\widetilde{T}_{0,\kappa}(n)

for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}
.
More precisely, given any small
\varepsilon_{0}>0
,
there exists
C>0
such that

(6.4)

\displaystyle\big|\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)-n^{2\kappa}\big|

\displaystyle\leq n^{2\kappa}|\mathfrak{h}(\delta,n)|+C\delta^{\varepsilon_{0}}|n|^{2\kappa+\varepsilon_{0}},

(6.5)

\displaystyle\big|\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa}(n)-n^{2\kappa}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{\varepsilon_{0}}|n|^{2\kappa+\varepsilon_{0}}

for any
0<\delta\leq\infty
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}
,
where
\mathfrak{h}(\delta,n)
is as in Lemma
2.3
(iv).
Moreover, given
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<\delta<\infty
, we have

(6.6)

\displaystyle|n|^{2\kappa-1}\lesssim_{\delta}\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)\lesssim\min(\delta^{-1}|n|^{2\kappa-1},n^{2\kappa})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa}(n)\sim|n|^{k},

uniformly in
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}
,
where the implicit constant in the first bound is independent
of
0<\delta\leq 1
.

Proof.

From (
1.34
) and (
2.6
), we have

(6.7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=1\\
\text{odd}\end{subarray}}^{2\kappa-1}\widetilde{a}_{2\kappa-1,\ell}\,\delta^{\ell-1}\mathfrak{L}_{\delta}^{\ell}(n)n^{2\kappa-\ell-1},\\
\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa}(n)&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=0\\
\text{even}\end{subarray}}^{2\kappa}\widetilde{a}_{2\kappa,\ell}\,\delta^{\ell}\mathfrak{L}_{\delta}^{\ell}(n)n^{2\kappa-\ell}.\end{split}

As for
\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa}(n)
,
the contribution from
\ell=0
is
the only term which survives in the limit as
\delta\to 0
,
which yields (
6.3
) in view of
the normalization (
1.18
).
As for
\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)
,
the contribution from
\ell=1
is
the only term which survives in the limit as
\delta\to 0
,
which yields (
6.3
) in view of
Lemma
2.3
and
the normalization (
1.18
).
The bounds (
6.4
) and (
6.5
) follow
from (
6.7
),
(
1.18
), and Lemma
2.3
.

The upper bounds in (
6.6
) follows
from (
6.7
) and Lemma
2.3
(i).
As for the lower bounds in (
6.6
),
it follows from (
6.7
), (
1.18
), the positivity of the constants

\widetilde{a}_{k,\ell}
(see (
4.59
)),
and Lemma
2.3
(iii) that

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)\geq 3\mathfrak{L}_{\delta}(n)n^{2\kappa-2}

\displaystyle\geq\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\delta}|n|^{2\kappa-1},&\delta|n|\gg 1,\\
n^{2\kappa},&\delta|n|\lesssim 1\end{cases}

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{\delta}|n|^{2\kappa-1},

uniformly in
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}
,
where the implicit constant is independent
of
0<\delta\leq 1
.
On the other hand, from (
6.7
), we have

\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa}(n)\geq n^{2\kappa}
.
Therefore, we obtain (
6.6
).
∎

We now present a proof of Proposition
6.1
.
While
it follows from the lines in the proof of Proposition 4.1 in
[
64
]
,
the argument on singularity / equivalence presented below is more involved due to the
more complex nature of the multiplier
\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}
in (
1.34
).

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

(i)
With Lemma
6.2
,
the bounds (
6.1
) and (
6.2
) follow
from a slight modification of the proof of Proposition
5.1
(i).

(ii)
From Lemma
6.2

(with
\varepsilon_{0}<2\varepsilon
)
and Lemma
2.3
(iv), we have

(6.8)

\displaystyle\langle n\rangle^{2\kappa-2-2\varepsilon}\frac{|n^{2\kappa}-\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)|}{\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)n^{2\kappa}}\lesssim\frac{\mathfrak{h}(\delta,n)}{\langle n\rangle^{1+2\varepsilon}}+\frac{\delta^{\varepsilon_{0}}}{\langle n\rangle^{1+2\varepsilon-\varepsilon_{0}}}\longrightarrow 0,

as
\delta\to 0
,
for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}
.
Moreover, we have

\eqref{T7}\lesssim\langle n\rangle^{-1-2\varepsilon+\varepsilon_{0}}

for any
0<\delta\leq 1
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}
.
Hence, from the dominated convergence theorem, we have

(6.9)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\eqref{T7}=0.

Similarly,
we have

(6.10)

\displaystyle\langle n\rangle^{2\kappa-1-2\varepsilon}\frac{|n^{2\kappa}-\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa}(n)|}{\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa}(n)n^{2\kappa}}\lesssim\frac{\delta^{\varepsilon_{0}}}{\langle n\rangle^{1+2\varepsilon-\varepsilon_{0}}}\longrightarrow 0,

as
\delta\to 0
,
for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}
.
Hence, from the dominated convergence theorem, we have

(6.11)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\eqref{T8}=0.

Then, proceeding as in (
5.8
) with (
6.9
) and (
6.11
),
we obtain the desired
L^{p}
-convergence of

\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
to
\widetilde{X}^{0}_{\lceil\frac{k}{2}\rceil}
as
\delta\to 0

in
W^{\frac{k-1}{2}-\varepsilon,r}(\mathbb{T})
,
which in turn implies weak convergence of

\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}

to
\widetilde{\mu}^{0}_{\lceil\frac{k}{2}\rceil}
.

(iii)
As in the proof of Proposition
5.1
(iii),
given
0<\delta<\infty
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, set

\displaystyle A_{n}^{\delta}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Re g_{n}}{(\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(n))^{\frac{1}{2}}},\quad A^{\delta}_{-n}=-\frac{\Im g_{n}}{(\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(n))^{\frac{1}{2}}},\quad B_{n}=\frac{\Re g_{n}}{|n|^{\lceil\frac{k}{2}\rceil}},\quad B_{-n}=-\frac{\Im g_{n}}{|n|^{\lceil\frac{k}{2}\rceil}}

such that

\displaystyle a^{\delta}_{\pm n}=\mathbb{E}[(A^{\delta}_{\pm n})^{2}]=(\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(n))^{-1}\qquad\text{and}\qquad b_{\pm n}=\mathbb{E}[B^{2}_{\pm n}]=|n|^{-2\lceil\frac{k}{2}\rceil}.

In the following, we consider the cases
k=2\kappa-1
and
k=2\kappa

such that
\lceil\frac{k}{2}\rceil=\kappa
.

Let
k=2\kappa-1
.
From (
6.7
), (
1.18
),
and Lemma
2.3
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{b_{n}}{a^{\delta}_{n}}-1

\displaystyle=\frac{\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)-n^{2\kappa}}{n^{2\kappa}}=-\mathfrak{h}(\delta,n)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=3\\
\text{odd}\end{subarray}}^{2\kappa-1}\widetilde{a}_{2\kappa-1,\ell}\,\delta^{\ell-1}\mathfrak{L}_{\delta}^{\ell}(n)n^{-\ell-1}

\displaystyle=-\mathfrak{h}(\delta,n)+O(\delta^{-1}|n|^{-1}).

Thus, from (
5.26
) and
Lemma
2.3
(iv), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\bigg(\frac{b_{n}}{a^{\delta}_{n}}-1\bigg)^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\mathfrak{h}^{2}(\delta,n)-C\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\frac{1}{\delta^{2}|n|^{2}}=\infty.

Hence, from Kakutani’s theorem (Lemma
2.9
),
we conclude that
\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta}_{\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}

and
\widetilde{\mu}^{0}_{\kappa}
are singular.

Next, we consider the case

k=2\kappa
.
From (
6.7
),
Lemma
2.3
(iii),
and the positivity of the coefficients
\widetilde{a}_{2\kappa,\ell}
,
we have

\displaystyle\frac{b_{n}}{a^{\delta}_{n}}-1=\frac{\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa}(n)-n^{2\kappa}}{n^{2\kappa}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=2\\
\text{even}\end{subarray}}^{2\kappa}\widetilde{a}_{2\kappa,\ell}\,\delta^{\ell}\mathfrak{L}_{\delta}^{\ell}(n)n^{-\ell}\gtrsim 1

for
|n|\gg\delta^{-1}
.
Hence, from Kakutani’s theorem (Lemma
2.9
),
we conclude that
\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta}_{\kappa}

and
\widetilde{\mu}^{0}_{\kappa}
are singular.

(iv)
Fix
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<\delta_{1}<\delta_{2}<\infty
.
In view of Kakutani’s theorem (Lemma
2.9
),
the claimed
singularity of
\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta_{1}}_{\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}
and
\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta_{2}}_{\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}
and equivalence of
\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta_{1}}_{\kappa}
and
\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta_{2}}_{\kappa}

follows once we prove

(6.12)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\bigg(\frac{\widetilde{T}_{\delta_{1},\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)-\widetilde{T}_{\delta_{2},\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)}{\widetilde{T}_{\delta_{1},\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)}\bigg)^{2}=\infty

and

(6.13)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\bigg(\frac{\widetilde{T}_{\delta_{1},\kappa}(n)-\widetilde{T}_{\delta_{2},\kappa}(n)}{\widetilde{T}_{\delta_{1},\kappa}(n)}\bigg)^{2}<\infty.

We first show (
6.12
). From (
6.7
) and (
2.6
), for any
\delta>0
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)

\displaystyle=\delta^{-1}n^{2\kappa-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=1\\
\text{odd}\end{subarray}}^{2\kappa-1}\widetilde{a}_{2\kappa-1,\ell}(\coth(\delta n)-(\delta n)^{-1})^{\ell},

from which we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)}{n^{2\kappa-1}}=\delta^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=1\\
\text{odd}\end{subarray}}^{2\kappa-1}\widetilde{a}_{2\kappa-1,\ell}.

This in turn implies

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\bigg(\frac{\widetilde{T}_{\delta_{1},\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)-\widetilde{T}_{\delta_{2},\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)}{\widetilde{T}_{\delta_{1},\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}(n)}\bigg)^{2}=\frac{(\delta_{2}-\delta_{1})^{2}}{\delta_{2}^{2}}\neq 0,

yielding (
6.12
).

Next, we show (
6.13
).
From (
6.7
) and (
2.6
), for any
\delta>0
, we have

(6.14)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\kappa}(n)&=n^{2\kappa}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=0\\
\text{even}\end{subarray}}^{2\kappa}\widetilde{a}_{2\kappa,\ell}\big(\coth(\delta n)-(\delta n)^{-1}\big)^{\ell}.\end{split}

Then, from (
6.14
) with (
1.18
), we have

(6.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&n^{-2\kappa}\big(\widetilde{T}_{\delta_{1},\kappa}(n)-\widetilde{T}_{\delta_{2},\kappa}(n)\big)\\
&\quad=\bigg(\coth(\delta_{1}n)-\coth(\delta_{2}n)-\frac{\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}}{\delta_{1}\delta_{2}n}\bigg)\\
&\quad\quad\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=2\\
\text{even}\end{subarray}}^{2\kappa}\widetilde{a}_{2\kappa,\ell}\sum_{j=0}^{\ell-1}\big(\coth(\delta_{1}n)-(\delta_{1}n)^{-1}\big)^{\ell-1-j}\big(\coth(\delta_{2}n)-(\delta_{2}n)^{-1}\big)^{j}.\end{split}

Combining (
6.14
) and (
6.15
), we have

(6.16)

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\widetilde{T}_{\delta_{1},\kappa}(n)-\widetilde{T}_{\delta_{2},\kappa}(n)}{\widetilde{T}_{\delta_{1},\kappa}(n)}\bigg)^{2}=C_{n}D^{2}_{n},

where
C_{n}
and
D_{n}
are given by

(6.17)

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{n}&=\big(\coth(\delta_{1}n)-\coth(\delta_{2}n)\big)^{2}+\frac{(\delta_{2}-\delta_{1})^{2}}{\delta_{1}^{2}\delta_{2}^{2}n^{2}}\\
&\quad-\frac{2(\delta_{2}-\delta_{1})\big(\coth(\delta_{1}n)-\coth(\delta_{2}n)\big)}{\delta_{1}\delta_{2}n},\\
D_{n}&=\frac{\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=2\\
\text{even}\end{subarray}}^{2\kappa}\widetilde{a}_{2\kappa,\ell}\sum_{j=0}^{\ell-1}\big(\coth(\delta_{1}n)-(\delta_{1}n)^{-1}\big)^{\ell-1-j}\big(\coth(\delta_{2}n)-(\delta_{2}n)^{-1}\big)^{j}}{\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=0\\
\text{even}\end{subarray}}^{2\kappa}\widetilde{a}_{2\kappa,\ell}\big(\coth(\delta_{1}n)-(\delta_{1}n)^{-1}\big)^{\ell}}.\end{split}

From (
6.17
), the positivity of the constants
\widetilde{a}_{2\kappa,\ell}
,
(
2.5
), and Lemma
2.2
,
we have

(6.18)

\displaystyle D_{n}\sim 1

for any
|n|\gg\delta_{1}^{-1}
.
On the other hand, from Cauchy’s inequality with
\delta_{2}>\delta_{1}>0
, we have

(6.19)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|C_{n}|&\lesssim_{\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}\frac{\max\big(e^{2(-\delta_{1}+\delta_{2})n},e^{2(\delta_{1}-\delta_{2})n}\big)}{(e^{\delta_{1}n}-e^{-\delta_{1}n})^{2}(e^{\delta_{2}n}-e^{-\delta_{2}n})^{2}}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\\
&\lesssim_{\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}\frac{1}{e^{2\delta_{1}|n|}}+\frac{1}{n^{2}},\end{split}

which is summable in
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}
.
Hence, (
6.13
) follows
from (
6.16
), (
6.18
), and (
6.19
).
∎

Remark 6.3
.

We point out that
for the summability of
the right-hand side of (
6.16
),
it is crucial to have
\delta_{1}>0
;
see the right-hand side of (
6.19
).
As we proved in Proposition
6.1
(ii),
(
6.13
) indeed fails
when
\delta_{1}=0
.

6.2.
Uniform integrability
of the shallow-water densities

In this subsection,
we establish the uniform (in
N
and also in
0<\delta\leq 1
)

L^{p}
-integrability of the truncated density
\widetilde{F}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}v)
,
where
\widetilde{F}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(v)
is as in (
1.36
):

(6.20)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{F}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(v)=\eta_{K}\big(\|v\|_{L^{2}}\big)\exp\Big(-\widetilde{R}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(v)\Big)\end{split}

for a fixed constant
K>0
.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
k\geq 2
be an integer and
K>0
.
Then, given any
0<\delta<\infty
and finite
p\geq 1
,
there exists
C_{\delta,p}>0
such that

(6.21)

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\|\widetilde{F}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\|\widetilde{F}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}v)\|_{L^{p}(d\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})}\leq C_{\delta,p}<\infty,

where the constant
C_{\delta,p}
is independent of

0<\delta\leq 1
.
Here,

\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
and

\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
are as in (
1.33
) and (
1.32
), respectively;
see also (
1.41
) and (
1.40
).
When
k
is even, (
6.21
) also holds
for
\delta=0
.

As in the deep-water case,
by defining

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(v)=\exp\Big(-\widetilde{R}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(v)-A\|v\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\Big),

we
see that Proposition
6.4
follows once we prove the following
bound on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}v)
.

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
k\geq 2
be an integer.
Then, given any
0<\delta<\infty
and finite
p\geq 1
,
there exist
A=A(p)>0
,
\alpha>0
, and
C_{\delta,p}>0
such that

(6.22)

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}=\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}v)\|_{L^{p}(d\widetilde{\mu}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})}\leq C_{\delta,p}<\infty,

where the constant
C_{\delta,p}
is independent of

0<\delta\leq 1
.
Here,
A=A(p)
is independent of
0<\delta\leq 1
, while

\alpha
is independent of
p
and
0<\delta<\infty
.
When
k
is even, (
6.22
) also holds
for
\delta=0
.

The remaining part of this subsection
is devoted to a proof of Proposition
6.5
.
Let us introduce some notations.
Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}

the Fourier multiplier operator
with multiplier
\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(n)
defined in (
1.34
).
Then,
we define a centered Gaussian process
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}

by setting

(6.23)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(t)=\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}W(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\frac{B_{n}(t)}{\big(\widetilde{T}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(n)\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}}e_{n},

where
W
is as in (
5.15
).
By comparing (
6.23
) with (
1.33
),
we have

\Law\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(1)\big)=\Law\big(\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}\big)
.
In the current shallow-water regime,
the Boué-Dupuis variational formula reads as follows.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
k\geq 2
be an integer,
0<\delta<\infty
,
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
Suppose that
G:C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})\to\mathbb{R}

is measurable such that
\mathbb{E}\big[|G(\mathbf{P}_{N}\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})|^{p}\big]<\infty

and
\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\exp\big(G(\mathbf{P}_{N}\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})\big)\big|^{q}\big]<\infty

for some
1<p,q<\infty
with
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=1
.
Then, we have

(6.24)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\log\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp\big(G(\mathbf{P}_{N}\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})\big)\Big]\\
&\quad=\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{H}_{a}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[G\big(\mathbf{P}_{N}\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(1)+\mathbf{P}_{N}\widetilde{I}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(\theta)(1)\big)-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\|\theta(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}dt\bigg],\end{split}

where

\widetilde{I}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(\theta)
is defined by

\displaystyle\widetilde{I}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(\theta)(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\theta(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}

and the expectation
\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}

is taken with respect to the underlying probability measure
\mathbb{P}
.
When
k
is even, (
6.24
) also holds
for
\delta=0

with
\widetilde{T}_{0,\frac{k}{2}}(n)=n^{k}
as in (
6.3
).

As in Subsection
5.2
,
with a slight abuse of notation,
we use

\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}
to denote

\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(1)

in applying the variational formula (
6.24
)
in the remaining part
of this subsection.

As in the deep-water case (see (
5.19
)), we have

(6.25)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{I}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2}}(\theta)(1)\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}_{x}}^{2}\leq\widetilde{a}_{\delta}\int_{0}^{t}\|\theta(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}dt

for any
\theta\in\mathbb{H}_{a}
,
where the constant
\widetilde{a}_{\delta}>0
is independent of
0<\delta\leq 1
.
When
k
is even, (
6.25
) also holds
for
\delta=0

with the constant
\widetilde{a}_{\delta}
independent of

0\leq\delta\leq 1
.

As in Subsection
5.2
,
our main task is to prove the following lemma on the interaction potential

\widetilde{R}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(v)
,
which plays a role of
Lemma
5.7

in the deep-water regime.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
k\geq 2
be an integer.
Given
0<\delta<\infty
and small
\varepsilon_{0}>0
,
there exist
\alpha\geq 1
, independent of
\delta
and
\varepsilon_{0}
, and

C_{\delta,\varepsilon_{0}}>0
such that

(6.26)

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[\widetilde{R}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\big]\big|\leq C_{\delta,\varepsilon_{0}}+\mathbb{E}\Big[C_{\delta,\varepsilon_{0}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}+\varepsilon_{0}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{2}\Big],

uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
where the constant
C_{\delta,\varepsilon_{0}}>0

is independent of
0<\delta\leq 1
.
Here,

(6.27)

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{N}:=\mathbf{P}_{N}\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Theta_{N}=\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta

as in (
5.21
)
and, in particular, they have mean zero on
\mathbb{T}
.
When
k
is even, (
6.26
) also holds
for
\delta=0
.

Once we prove Lemma
6.7
,
arguing as in the proof of Proposition
5.4
in Subsection
5.2

with (
6.25
) and (
6.26
),
we can prove Proposition
6.5

(and hence Proposition
6.4
).
More precisely,
as in (
5.20
),
define

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2},p,N}(\Theta)

by setting

(6.28)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2},p,N}(\Theta)&=\mathbb{E}\bigg[-p\widetilde{R}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}}(\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N})\\
&\hphantom{XXX}-pA\|\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{a}_{\delta}^{-1}\|\Theta\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}\bigg]\end{split}

for
\Theta\in H^{\frac{k}{2}}_{0}(\mathbb{T})
, where we used the notations in (
6.27
).
Then,
Proposition
6.5
follows once
we establish an
upper bound on

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2},p,N}(\Theta)

for each
0<\delta<\infty
,
uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\Theta\in H^{\frac{k}{2}}_{0}(\mathbb{T})
and also
uniformly in
0<\delta\leq 1
.
It follows from (
6.28
), Lemma
6.7
,
(
5.27
) (with
X^{\delta}_{N}
replaced by
\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{N}
),
and Proposition
6.1
(i) that given any small
\varepsilon_{0}>0
, we have

(6.29)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2},p,N}(\Theta)&\leq p(1+A)\widetilde{C}_{\delta,\varepsilon_{0},\alpha}+\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Cp\varepsilon_{0}-\tfrac{1}{2}\widetilde{a}_{\delta}^{-1}\big)\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{2}\\
&\quad+\big(pC_{\delta,\varepsilon_{0}}-\tfrac{1}{2}pA\big)\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\Big],\end{split}

uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}

and
\Theta\in H^{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbb{T})
,
where
\alpha\geq 1
is as in Lemma
6.25

and the constants

\widetilde{C}_{\delta,\varepsilon_{0},\alpha}

and

C_{\delta,\varepsilon_{0}}
are independent of

0<\delta\leq 1
(but depend on
\varepsilon_{0}>0
).
Hence, the desired bound on

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\delta,\frac{k}{2},p,N}(\Theta)

(and hence
Proposition
6.5
)
follows once we choose
\varepsilon_{0},A>0
in (
6.29
)
such that

Cp\varepsilon_{0}-\tfrac{1}{2}\widetilde{a}_{\delta}^{-1}<0\qquad\text{and}\qquad pC_{\delta,\varepsilon_{0}}-\tfrac{1}{2}pA<0.

Since

\widetilde{a}_{\delta}
in (
6.25
) is independent of

0<\delta\leq 1
,
we can first choose
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(p)>0
,
independently of

0<\delta\leq 1
,
which in turn allow us to choose

A=A(p)>0

independently of

0<\delta\leq 1
.

It remains to prove Lemma
6.7
.
Before proceeding to a proof of Lemma
6.7
,
we first state a preliminary lemma
which is a shallow-water analogue of Lemma
5.9
.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
k\geq 2
be an integer.
Let
0\leq\delta<\infty

and

s,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
with
\sigma_{1}\leq\sigma_{2}\leq\frac{k}{2}

such that

2k-2\sigma_{1}-2\sigma_{2}>1
and
(
5.29
) holds.
Then, given any finite
p\geq 1
,
1\leq r\leq\infty
, and
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we have

(6.30)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\big\|\mathbf{P}_{\neq 0}\big((\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{\sigma_{1}}(\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta})^{\alpha_{1}}\mathbf{P}_{N}\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})(\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{\sigma_{2}}(\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta})^{\alpha_{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N}\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{\frac{k}{2}})\big)\big\|_{W^{s,r}}^{p}\bigg]<C_{\delta}<\infty,

uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
where the constant
C_{\delta}>0
is independent of
0\leq\delta\leq 1
.
The bound (
6.30
) also holds
even if we replace

\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{\sigma_{j}}
by
\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{\sigma_{j}-\kappa_{j}}\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{j}}

for any
\kappa_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
with
\kappa_{j}\leq\sigma_{j}
,
j=1,2
.

Proof.

When
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}=0
,
the bound (
6.30
) follows
from a slight modification of the proof of Lemma
5.9

with (
1.33
) and (
6.6
) in
Lemma
6.2
(which gives the same lower bound
|n|^{k}
as in the deep-water case).
Otherwise, we proceed as in
the proof of Lemma
5.9

and then use the bound

|\delta\widehat{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}_{\delta}(n)|\lesssim 1

which follows from (
2.6
) and Lemma
2.3
.
∎

We now present a proof of Lemma
6.7
.

Proof of Lemma
6.7
.

We only consider
0<\delta<\infty
.
For the
\delta=0
case with even
k
, see Remark
6.10
.
We separately treat the cases
k=2\kappa
and
k=2\kappa-1
,
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
.
In the following, all the estimates hold uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}

and
all the constants are understood to be uniform in
0<\delta\leq 1
.

\bullet

Case 1:

k=2\kappa-1
is odd.

From Proposition
6.1

(see also Remark
5.2
),
we know that
\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{N}
belongs
to
W^{\kappa-1-\varepsilon,\infty}(\mathbb{T})\setminus W^{\kappa-1,1}(\mathbb{T})
,
almost surely (in the limiting sense as
N\to\infty
).
Namely, if a monomial
p(v)

appearing on the right-hand side of (
4.92
)
has two factors with
\kappa-1
derivatives,
we need to rely on a probabilistic estimate
(as in Lemma
5.7
).
Otherwise, by (fractional) integration by parts,
we may assume that all the factors in
p(v)

has at most
\kappa-\frac{3}{2}
derivatives
and we can proceed deterministically
(as in Lemma
5.6
).

Let
\widetilde{R}_{i}
,
i=1,2
, be the
i
th term on the right-hand side of (
4.92
).
We consider the first term
\widetilde{R}_{1}
.
From Lemma
2.4
(ii), we have

(6.31)

\displaystyle|\delta\widehat{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}_{\delta}(n)|\lesssim 1\qquad\text{and}\qquad|\widehat{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}_{\delta}(n)|\lesssim|n|.

Moreover, the first bound
is equivalence for

|n|\gg\delta^{-1}
,
while
the second bound
is equivalence for

|n|\lesssim\delta^{-1}
.
See also
Lemma
2.4
(iii).
Any monomial in
\widetilde{R}_{1}
satisfies

\#\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}=\#\delta+1
, namely,
there is exactly one factor of
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}
which is not paired with
\delta
,
causing a derivative loss in view of (
6.31
).
Hence, although we have
\#\partial_{x}\leq 2\kappa+3-2j

for
\widetilde{R}_{1}
,
by accounting for this derivative loss,
we virtually need to work with a new constraint

\#\partial_{x}\leq 2\kappa+4-2j
.
33
33

33

For simplicity of the presentation,
we simply replace such unpaired
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}
by
\partial_{x}
in the following.

In particular, when
j=\#v=3
, we have
\#\partial_{x}=2\kappa-2

such that there can be two factors with
\kappa-1
derivatives
(which is
also the case for any cubic monomial in
\widetilde{R}_{2}

under
\#\partial_{x}\leq 2\kappa+1-j
(with
j=3
)).

Consequently, in order to prove (
6.26
)
for
\widetilde{R}^{\delta}_{\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}=\widetilde{R}_{1}+\widetilde{R}_{2}
,
it suffices to consider a monomial
p(v)
whose fundamental form
\widetilde{p}(v)
is given by

(6.32)

(a)
\widetilde{p}(v)=v\partial_{x}^{\kappa-1}v\partial_{x}^{\kappa-1}v

and  (b)
\widetilde{p}(v)=\prod_{i=1}^{j}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{i}}v

for

(6.33)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&j=3,\ldots,2\kappa+1,\quad 0\leq\alpha_{j}\leq\cdots\leq\alpha_{1}\leq\kappa-1\quad\text{such that}\\
&(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\neq(\kappa-1,\kappa-1),\quad\text{and}\quad\\
&\alpha_{1\cdots j}\leq\max(2\kappa+4-2j,2\kappa+1-j)=2\kappa+1-j.\end{split}

\bullet

Subcase 1.a:

\widetilde{p}(v)=v\partial_{x}^{\kappa-1}v\partial_{x}^{\kappa-1}v
.

Since
k=2\kappa-1
, we have

\widetilde{p}(v)=v\partial_{x}^{\frac{k-1}{2}}v\partial_{x}^{\frac{k-1}{2}}v
.
Recall that,
in Case 2 of the proof of Lemma
5.7
,
we treated

p(u)=u\partial_{x}^{m}u\partial_{x}^{m}u
with
m=\frac{k-1}{2}

(see (
5.45
)-(
5.47
)).
Suppose that
p(v)=v\partial_{x}^{\frac{k-1}{2}}v\partial_{x}^{\frac{k-1}{2}}v
,
namely,
\#\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}=0
.
Then, proceeding as in
(
5.45
)-(
5.47
)
but with Lemma
6.8
instead of
Lemma
5.9
,
we obtain (
6.26
).

In general, in view of the
L^{r}
-boundedness of
\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}

for
1<r<\infty
(Lemma
2.4
(iii)),
we can simply drop
any occurrence of
\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}
,
just as we dropped the Hilbert transform
\mathcal{H}

and the perturbation operator
\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}

in the proofs of Lemmas
5.6
and
5.7
.
Then, proceeding as above,
we obtain (
6.26
).

\bullet

Subcase 1.b:

\widetilde{p}(v)=\prod_{i=1}^{j}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{i}}v
as in (b) of (
6.32
), satisfying
(
6.33
).

In this case, from Hölder’s inequality and (
2.14
) in Lemma
2.4
(iii), we have
34
34

34

As mentioned in the proofs
of Lemmas
5.6
and
5.7
, the
W^{\alpha_{i},\infty}
-norm should really be
the
W^{\alpha_{i},r}
-norm for
r\gg 1
which can then be controlled by
the
W^{\alpha_{i}+\varepsilon,\infty}
-norm. We, however, suppress this point in the following.

(6.34)

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{T}}p(v)dx\bigg|\leq C_{\delta}\|u\|_{H^{\alpha_{1}}}\|v\|_{H^{\alpha_{2}}}\prod_{i=3}^{j}\|v\|_{W^{\alpha_{i},\infty}},

where
C_{\delta}
is independent of
0<\delta\leq 1
.
Then,
we can proceed as in Case 2 of the proof of Lemma
5.6

by noting the following two points:
(i) after partial integration by parts, we may assume that

\alpha_{i}\leq\kappa-\frac{3}{2}=\frac{k-2}{2}
for any
i=1,\dots,j

such that (
5.35
) holds,
and
(ii) under the condition

\alpha_{1\cdots j}\leq 2\kappa+1-j
with
j\geq 3
, we have

(6.35)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\gamma=\frac{2}{k}\bigg(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\sum_{i=3}^{j}\Big(\alpha_{i}+\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon\Big)\bigg)\leq\frac{2}{k}\bigg(k+1-\frac{1}{2}j+(j-2)\varepsilon\bigg)<2,\end{split}

and thus the bound (
5.37
) holds with
\gamma
in (
6.35
).
Hence, the bound (
6.26
) in this case follows from substituting

v=\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{N}+\Theta_{N}
into
the left-hand side of (
6.34
)
and applying (
5.35
), (
5.37
), Young’s inequality,
and Proposition
6.1
(i).

\bullet

Case 2:

k=2\kappa
is even.

We now estimate
\widetilde{R}^{\delta}_{\kappa}(v)
in (
4.92
).
In this case, we have
\#\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}\leq\#\delta
,
namely, every
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}
is paired with
\delta
in each monomial.
In view of the discussion in Case 1,
we may simply drop occurrences of
\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}
.
Hence, under the condition
\#\partial_{x}\leq 2\kappa+2-j
,

|p(v)|\leq\kappa
, and
j\geq 3
,
it suffices to consider a monomial
p(v)
whose fundamental form
\widetilde{p}(v)
is given by

(6.36)

(a)
\widetilde{p}(v)=v\partial_{x}^{\kappa-1}v\partial_{x}^{\kappa}v

and  (b)
\widetilde{p}(v)=\prod_{i=1}^{j}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{i}}v

for

(6.37)

\displaystyle j=3,\ldots,2\kappa+2,\quad 0\leq\alpha_{i}\leq\kappa-1,i=1,\dots,j,\quad\text{such that}\quad\alpha_{1\cdots j}\leq 2\kappa+2-j.

\bullet

Subcase 2.a:
